=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an array nums of length n. You are also given an integer k.
You perform the following operation on nums once:

Select a subarray nums[i..j] where 0 <= i <= j <= n - 1.
Select an integer x and add x to all the elements in nums[i..j].

Find the maximum frequency of the value k after the operation.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [1,2,3,4,5,6], k = 1
Output: 2
Explanation:
After adding -5 to nums[2..5], 1 has a frequency of 2 in [1, 2, -2, -1, 0, 1].

Example 2:

Input: nums = [10,2,3,4,5,5,4,3,2,2], k = 10
Output: 4
Explanation:
After adding 8 to nums[1..9], 10 has a frequency of 4 in [10, 10, 11, 12, 13, 13, 12, 11, 10, 10].

 
Constraints:

1 <= n == nums.length <= 10^5
1 <= nums[i] <= 50
1 <= k <= 50

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxFrequency(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

We have array nums length n up to 1e5, values 1..50, k 1..50. Operation: choose any contiguous subarray and add integer x (can be any integer, likely positive/negative/zero) to all elements in that subarray. Need maximize frequency of value k after operation.

We can add same x to a contiguous segment. For an element nums[p] to become k after operation, need:
- if p outside selected subarray: nums[p] == k.
- if p inside selected subarray: nums[p] + x == k, so x = k - nums[p].
Since x is constant for entire subarray, all elements inside selected subarray that become k must have same original value v = k - x. Elements with other values inside subarray won't become k (unless also equal v? Actually if x = k - nums[p], for each p inside, final = nums[p]+x = nums[p]+k - v. To equal k requires nums[p]=v). So selected subarray contributes count of occurrences of some value v inside it. Outside contributes original k count.

Let's maybe think if there is an alternative DP with 3 states due to operation can add x to subarray, and x can be chosen to maximize frequency of k. But our reduction is simpler because values small. Let's confirm with known LeetCode 3003? I recall a solution:
class Solution:
    def maxFrequency(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        ans = nums.count(k)
        for target in range(1, 51):
            if target == k: continue
            cur = 0
            for a in nums:
                if a == target:
                    cur += 1
                elif a == k:
                    cur -= 1
                if cur < 0:
                    cur = 0
                ans = max(ans, nums.count(k) + cur)
        return ans
Maybe they update ans inside loop with base + cur. But if cur is current max ending here? Actually if cur = max(0, cur+delta), then cur is max subarray sum ending at current position. The global max is max over cur. If we update ans with base+cur each step, that's effectively global max because cur at each step is max ending here; max over steps is global max. So OK. But if we only update after each v with final cur, not enough. Need best variable. In code, can update ans inside loop.

Let's write:
class Solution:
    def maxFrequency(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        base = nums.count(k)
        best_gain = 0
        for v in range(1, 51):
            if v == k:
                continue
            cur = 0
            best = 0
            for num in nums:
                if num == v:
                    cur += 1
                elif num == k:
                    cur -= 1
                if cur < 0:
                    cur = 0
                if cur > best:
                    best = cur
            if best > best_gain:
                best_gain = best
        return base + best_gain

Need ensure if num neither and cur=0, best unchanged. OK.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible hidden test where `nums` contains
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxFrequency(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        base = nums.count(k)
        best_gain = 0

        for v in range(1, 51):
            if v == k:
                continue

            cur = 0
            best = 0

            for num in nums:
                if num == v:
                    cur += 1
                elif num == k:
                    cur -= 1

                if cur < 0:
                    cur = 0
                if cur > best:
                    best = cur

            if best > best_gain:
                best_gain = best

        return base + best_gain
```
